## Behavioral explanation
Constructs a **two-part tagged record** in the caller's buffer (`param_1`). It writes a fixed **8-byte magic** (`0x2588d0`) as the record header, appends a 5-byte formatted field (`0x2588e0`), then at sub-offset `+0x10` writes a 2-byte sub-tag (`0x2588e8`), fills the sub-record from `param_3` (`fill_subrecord`), and appends a trailing field (`0x2588f0`). The 8-byte magic at `0x2588d0` is the **same signature scanned for by `find_tagged_record` (0x0017c920)** — this pair builds and later locates records of one type. The exact record purpose (a session/connection descriptor, a serialized request) is unresolved without the tag bytes.

## Input / output
- `param_1` : `void*` destination record buffer (main part at +0x00, sub-record at +0x10).
- `param_2` : unused in body (present in signature; likely a length/context passed through by ABI).
- `param_3` : source for the sub-record fill (`fill_subrecord`).
- **return** : void.

## Side effects / control flow (linear, no branches)
1. `blob_copy_n(param_1, 0x2588d0, 8)` — write 8-byte magic header.
2. `field_format(stack, 0x2588e0, 5)` then `field_append(param_1, stack)` — append 5-byte formatted field.
3. `blob_copy_n(param_1+0x10, 0x2588e8, 2)` — sub-record 2-byte tag.
4. `fill_subrecord(param_1+0x10, param_3)` — populate sub-record from param_3.
5. `field_append(param_1+0x10, 0x2588f0)` — append trailing field.

## Constants & flags
- `0x2588d0` — 8-byte record **magic** (shared with the 0x0017c920 scanner).
- `0x2588e8` — 2-byte sub-record tag.
- sub-record offset `+0x10` within the record.

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// Build a 2-part tagged record: 8-byte magic + field, then a sub-record at +0x10.
// The 8-byte magic 0x2588d0 is what find_tagged_record() (0x0017c920) matches on.
void build_tagged_record(void *rec, /*ctx*/ void *unused, void *src)
{
    blob_copy_n(rec, TAG_MAGIC8 /*0x2588d0*/, 8);       // FUN_0010a4f0

    u8 tmp[16];
    field_format(tmp, TAG_FIELD5 /*0x2588e0*/, 5);      // FUN_00109728
    field_append(rec, tmp);                             // FUN_00109ab0

    void *sub = (char*)rec + 0x10;
    blob_copy_n(sub, TAG_SUB2 /*0x2588e8*/, 2);
    fill_subrecord(sub, src);                           // FUN_0017c748
    field_append(sub, TAG_TAIL /*0x2588f0*/);
}
```

## Evidence
- Raw decompile FUN_0017c6b8.c; the 8-byte magic 0x2588d0 also appears as an 8-byte compare key in FUN_0017c920 (build↔find pairing).
- Confidence **Medium**: control flow exact; the record's semantic identity and the helper (`field_format`/`field_append`) exact behaviors are inferred, not proven.

## Remaining uncertainty
Byte contents/meaning of tags 0x2588d0/e0/e8/f0; whether `param_2` is truly unused; exact behavior of the field-builder helpers.

**Callers:** — (indirect)
**Callees:** FUN_00106910 (tag_match), FUN_0017c9e8 (record_value)
**Referenced globals:** const tags 0x258948 (1B), 0x2588d0 (8B magic)
**Referenced strings:** —
**Referenced opcodes:** —
**State vars:** —

## Behavioral explanation
Linear **scan/lookup** over a contiguous region of candidate pointers. Starting at `param_1` and stepping forward `param_2` times (stride 1 byte per the pointer arithmetic — i.e. it probes each successive address), it tests every candidate against a **1-byte tag** (`0x258948`) and the **8-byte record magic** (`0x2588d0`, the same signature emitted by `build_tagged_record` 0x0017c6b8). On a match it writes the matching pointer to `*param_3` and a derived value (`record_value(ptr)`) to `*param_4`. The loop does **not** break on first match, so it returns the **last** matching entry in the range. Out-params are pre-cleared, so "not found" yields `*param_3 = *param_4 = 0`.

## Input / output
- `param_1` : `int` base address / first candidate.
- `param_2` : `int` count of candidates to probe (must be `> 0`).
- `param_3` : `int*` out — matched record pointer (0 if none).
- `param_4` : `u32*` out — `record_value(match)` (0 if none).
- **return** : void (results via out-params).

## Important branches
- guard: `param_1 != 0 && param_2 > 0` — else both outs stay 0.
- per candidate `p`: `tag_match(p, 0x258948, 1) == 0` **and** `tag_match(p, 0x2588d0, 8) == 0` → record `*param_3 = p`, `*param_4 = record_value(p)`.
- no early break → last match wins; `p++`, `count--` until `count == 0`.

## Constants & flags
- `0x258948` — 1-byte discriminator tag.
- `0x2588d0` — 8-byte record magic (shared with the builder 0x0017c6b8).
- match convention: `tag_match == 0` means equal (memcmp-style).

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// Scan [base, base+count) for a record matching a 1-byte tag AND the 8-byte magic.
// Returns the LAST match (no early break) via out-params; (0,0) if none.
void find_tagged_record(char *base, int count, int *out_ptr, u32 *out_val)
{
    *out_val = 0;
    *out_ptr = 0;
    if (base != NULL && count > 0) {
        do {
            if (tag_match(base, TAG_DISC1 /*0x258948*/, 1) == 0 &&
                tag_match(base, TAG_MAGIC8 /*0x2588d0*/, 8) == 0) {
                *out_ptr = (int)base;
                *out_val = record_value(base);          // FUN_0017c9e8
            }
            count--;
            base++;
        } while (count != 0);
    }
}
```

## Evidence
- Raw decompile FUN_0017c920.c; the dual `FUN_00106910(...,8)==0 && (...,1)==0` match and no-break loop are explicit.
- The 8-byte magic 0x2588d0 is emitted by `build_tagged_record` (0x0017c6b8) → confirms build↔find pairing.
- Confidence **Medium**: control flow exact; `tag_match`/`record_value` semantics and the byte-stride (probing every byte offset vs. a struct stride) inferred.

## Remaining uncertainty
Whether the 1-byte forward stride is literal (byte-granular scan) or a decompiler artifact of a struct-stride loop; what `record_value` computes; the tag meanings.

# 0x0017f6d0 dbcman_transfer_0x318

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Original address | 0x0017f6d0 |
| Binary / overlay | SLES_533.19 |
| Module | main-ee |
| Original generated name | FUN_0017f6d0 |
| Resolved name | dbcman_transfer_0x318 |
| Subsystem | session |
| Relevance | core |
| Status | reconstructed |
| Confidence | High |
| Runtime validated | no |

**Callers:** — (indirect)
**Callees:** FUN_00115c68 (sif_rpc_call), FUN_0017fd00 (dbcman_errlog)
**Referenced globals:** dbcman client node 0x27af88, shared buffer 0x27b000 (arg0 @+0x00, arg1 @+0x04, in_len @+0x08, in_aux @+0x0c, in_data @+0x10, ret @+0x410, out_data @+0x210)
**Referenced strings:** errlog fmt 0x258b40
**Referenced opcodes:** —
**State vars:** —

## Behavioral explanation
Bidirectional dbcman **data transfer**, RPC **fno 0x80001318**. Marshals two context words (`param_1`,`param_2`), an **input length** (`*param_3`) and an **auxiliary count** (`*param_5`), plus an inline **input payload** (`*param_3` bytes copied from `param_4` into buffer `+0x10`). After the call it reads a signed result at `+0x410`; on success it returns the updated length/aux via the same out-params and copies **`out_count` bytes** of reply payload from buffer `+0x210` into `param_6`. This is the dbcman **send-and-receive** primitive (companion to `dbcman_transact` fno 0x319 and `dbcman_send_data2` fno 0x31b).

## Input / output
- `param_1`,`param_2` : `u32` context words → buffer `+0x00`,`+0x04`.
- `param_3` : `int*` in/out — **input byte length** on entry; on success set to returned `+0x08`.
- `param_4` : `const u8*` — input payload (`*param_3` bytes → buffer `+0x10`).
- `param_5` : `int*` in/out — auxiliary/**output count** hint on entry; on success set to returned `+0x0c` (actual out byte count).
- `param_6` : `u8*` out — receives `*param_5` bytes copied from buffer `+0x210`.
- **return** : signed result `iRam0027b410` (>=0 ok, <0 dbcman error); `0` if the RPC transport itself failed.

## Important branches
- copy loop: if `*param_3 > 0`, byte-copy `*param_3` bytes `param_4 → 0x27b010`.
- `sif_rpc_call < 0` → `dbcman_errlog(0x258b40)`, return `0`.
- else `ret = iRam0027b410`; if `ret >= 0`: write back `*param_3 = +0x08`, `*param_5 = +0x0c`; if `+0x0c > 0`, byte-copy `+0x0c` bytes `0x27b210 → param_6`.
- `ret < 0` → returned as-is (dbcman-level error, no output copy).

## Constants & flags
- `0x80001318` — dbcman RPC function number.
- `0x280` — shared buffer size; input payload at `+0x10`, output payload at `+0x210`, result at `+0x410`.

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// dbcman bidirectional transfer, RPC fno 0x318: send in_data, receive out_data.
int dbcman_transfer_0x318(u32 ctx0, u32 ctx1,
                          int *io_len,   const u8 *in_data,
                          int *io_count, u8 *out_data)
{
    dbc_buf.in_len = *io_len;                           // 0x27b008
    dbc_buf.in_aux = *io_count;                         // 0x27b00c
    dbc_buf.arg0   = ctx0;                              // 0x27b000
    dbc_buf.arg1   = ctx1;                              // 0x27b004
    for (int i = 0; i < *io_len; i++)                   // inline input payload
        ((u8*)0x27b010)[i] = in_data[i];

    long rc = sif_rpc_call(&dbc_client, 0x80001318, 0,
                           dbc_buf, 0x280, dbc_buf, 0x280, 0);
    if (rc < 0) { dbcman_errlog(FMT_0x318_err); return 0; }

    int ret = dbc_buf.result;                           // iRam0027b410
    if (ret >= 0) {
```
